Cross-section measurements of the Higgs boson decaying into a pair of τ \mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}} -leptons in proton–proton collisions at s = 13 TeV \sqrt{s}=$13\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV}$ with the ATLAS detector
The ATLAS Collaboration

Abstract
A measurement of production cross sections of the Higgs boson in proton–proton collisions is presented in the H → τ τ \mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}} decay channel. The analysis is performed using 36.1 fb − 1 36.1\text{\,}{\mathrm{fb}}^{-1} of data recorded by the ATLAS experiment at the Large Hadron Collider at a center-of-mass energy of s = 13 TeV \sqrt{s}=$13\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV}$ . All combinations of leptonic ( τ → ℓ ν ν ¯ \mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\to\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\nu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{\nu}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}} with ℓ = e , μ \mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}=\mathit{{e}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}},\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}} ) and hadronic ( τ → hadrons ν \mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\to\text{hadrons}\penalty\ \mathit{{\nu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}} ) τ \mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}} decays are considered. The H → τ τ \mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}} signal over the expected background from other Standard Model processes is established with an observed (expected) significance of 4.4 (4.1) standard deviations. Combined with results obtained using data taken at 7 and 8 TeV 8\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV} center-of-mass energies, the observed (expected) significance amounts to 6.4 (5.4) standard deviations and constitutes an observation of H → τ τ \mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}} decays. Using the data taken at s = 13 TeV \sqrt{s}=$13\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV}$ , the total cross section in the H → τ τ \mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}} decay channel is measured to be 3.76558372941 − 0.591224858352 + 0.601597224288 ​ (stat.) − 0.74335289208 + 0.874736193936 ​ (syst.) ​ pb $3.76558372941$\,^{+$0.601597224288$}_{-$0.591224858352$}\,\text{(stat.)}\,^{+$0.874736193936$}_{-$0.74335289208$}\,\text{(syst.)}\,$\mathrm{pb}$ , for a Higgs boson of mass 125 GeV 125\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV} assuming the relative contributions of its production modes as predicted by the Standard Model. Total cross sections in the H → τ τ \mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}} decay channel are determined separately for vector-boson-fusion production and gluon–gluon-fusion production to be σ H → τ τ VBF = 0.2800453818 ± 0.09 (stat.) + 0.106 − 0.093 (syst.) pb \sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{VBF}}=$0.2800453818$\pm$0.09$\,\text{(stat.)}\,^{+$0.106$}_{-$0.093$}\,\text{(syst.)}\,$\mathrm{pb}$ and σ H → τ τ g ​ g ​ F = 3.096016028 ± 1.0 (stat.) + 1.63 − 1.258 (syst.) pb \sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}}=$3.096016028$\pm$1.0$\,\text{(stat.)}\,^{+$1.63$}_{-$1.258$}\,\text{(syst.)}\,$\mathrm{pb}$ , respectively. Similarly, results of a fit are reported in the framework of simplified template cross sections. All measurements are in agreement with Standard Model expectations.

1
Introduction

The ATLAS and CMS collaborations discovered
[
1
,
2
]
a particle consistent with the
Standard Model (SM)
[
3
,
4
,
5
]
Higgs boson
[
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
]
in
2012.
Several properties of this particle, such as its coupling strengths, spin and charge-parity (CP) quantum numbers, were
studied with 7 and
8\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV}
center-of-mass energy (
\sqrt{s}
) proton–proton
collision data delivered by the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) in 2011 and 2012,
respectively, referred to as

?
Run 1
?
.
These results rely predominantly on studies of the bosonic decay modes
[
11
,
12
,
13
,
14
]

and have not shown any significant deviations from the SM expectations.

The coupling of the Higgs boson to the fermionic sector has
been established with the observation of the
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decay mode with a
signal significance of 5.5
\sigma
from a combination of ATLAS and CMS results
[
15
,
16
,
17
]

using LHC Run-1 data.
A measurement performed by the CMS Collaboration with Run-2 data at
\sqrt{s}=$13\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV}$
reached a significance of 4.9
\sigma

using
35.9\text{\,}{\mathrm{fb}}^{-1}
of integrated luminosity and 5.9
\sigma
combined with data from Run 1
[
18
]
.
While the Higgs-boson coupling to
other fermions such as top quarks
[
19
,
20
]
and bottom quarks
[
21
,
22
]
have been observed,
only upper limits exist on its coupling to muons
[
23
,
24
]

and the
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decay mode has been the only accessible leptonic decay
mode. It was also used to
constrain CP violation in the production via vector-boson fusion (
VBF
)
[
25
]

and is unique in that it provides sensitivity to CP violation in the Higgs-boson coupling to leptons
[
26
]
.

This paper presents cross-section times branching-fraction measurements of Higgs bosons that decay into a pair of
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-leptons in proton–proton (
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
) collisions at
\sqrt{s}=$13\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV}$
using data collected by the ATLAS experiment in 2015 and 2016,
corresponding to an integrated luminosity of
36.1\text{\,}{\mathrm{fb}}^{-1}
.
All combinations of leptonic (
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\to\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\nu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{\nu}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
with
\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}=\mathit{{e}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}},\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
) and hadronic (
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\to\text{hadrons}\ \mathit{{\nu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
)
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decays
are considered.
1
1

1

Throughout this paper
the inclusion of charge-conjugate decay modes is implied.
The symbol
\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
is used to denote electrons and muons, also referred to as

?
light leptons
?
.

The corresponding three analysis channels are denoted by
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
,

\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and are composed of different dominant
backgrounds. While
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
is a dominant background in all channels, the
relative contributions from other backgrounds from top-quark and
other vector-boson decays, as well as from misidentified leptonic or hadronic
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decays, vary considerably between the channels.
Two analysis categories
are defined that are predominantly sensitive to Higgs bosons produced via
VBF
and
gluon–gluon fusion (
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
). A maximum-likelihood fit is performed on data
using distributions of the reconstructed di-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mass in signal regions (SRs), simultaneously with event
yields from control regions (CRs) that are included to constrain
normalizations of major backgrounds estimated from simulation.
The dominant and irreducible
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background is
estimated from simulation. This is different from the search for
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decays in Run 1
[
15
]
, which used the embedding
technique
[
27
]
. A reliable modeling of this background is therefore
of crucial importance for this analysis. Validation regions (VRs) based on
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events are studied, but not included in the fit,
to verify as precisely as possible the modeling of the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background.

The paper is organized as follows.
Section

2
describes the
ATLAS detector. This is followed in
Section

3

by a description of the dataset and Monte Carlo (MC) simulated samples employed
by this measurement.
Section

4
details the reconstruction
of particles and jets.
The event selection for each channel and event category as well as signal, control and validation regions are
discussed in
Section

5
. Background estimation techniques
and the systematic uncertainties of the analysis are described in

Sections

6
and
7
, respectively.
The signal extraction procedure and the results of the Higgs cross-section measurements in the

\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decay mode are presented in
Section

8
.

Section

9
gives the conclusions.

2
The ATLAS detector

The ATLAS experiment
[
28
]
at the LHC is a multipurpose particle
detector with a forward–backward symmetric cylindrical geometry and
a near-
4\pi
coverage in solid angle.
2
2

2

The ATLAS Collaboration uses a right-handed coordinate system with its origin at
the nominal interaction point (IP) in the center of the detector
and the
z
-axis along the beam pipe. The
x
-axis points from the IP to the
center of the LHC ring, and the
y
-axis points upwards. Cylindrical coordinates

(r,\phi)
are used in the transverse plane,
\phi
being the azimuthal angle
around the
z
-axis. The pseudorapidity is defined in terms of the polar angle

\theta
as
\eta=-\ln\tan(\theta/2)
. Angular distance
is measured in units of
\Delta R\equiv\sqrt{(\Delta\eta)^{2}+(\Delta\phi)^{2}}
.

It consists of an inner tracking detector
surrounded by a thin superconducting solenoid,
electromagnetic and hadron calorimeters, and a muon spectrometer.
The inner tracking detector covers the pseudorapidity range
|\eta|<2.5
.
It consists of a silicon pixel detector, which
has an additional innermost layer (positioned at a radial distance of
3.3\text{\,}\mathrm{cm}
from the beam line)
that was installed after Run 1
[
29
,
30
]
, and a
silicon microstrip detector surrounding the pixel detector, both
covering
|\eta|<2.5
, followed by a transition radiation straw-tube tracker
covering
|\eta|<2
.
The inner tracking detector is immersed in a
2\text{\,}\mathrm{T}
axial magnetic field provided
by the solenoid.
Lead/liquid-argon (LAr) sampling calorimeters provide electromagnetic (EM) energy measurements
with high granularity.
A hadron (steel/scintillator-tile) calorimeter covers the central pseudorapidity range (
|\eta|<1.7
).
The endcap and forward regions are instrumented with LAr calorimeters
for both the EM and hadronic energy measurements up to
|\eta|=4.9
.
The muon spectrometer surrounds the calorimeters and is based on three large air-core
toroidal superconducting magnets with eight coils each.
The field integral of the toroids ranges between
2.0
and
6.0\text{\,}\mathrm{T}\text{\,}\mathrm{m}

across most of the detector.
The muon spectrometer includes a system of precision tracking chambers and fast detectors for triggering.

Events are selected using a two-level trigger system. The
first-level trigger is implemented in hardware and uses a subset of the
detector information to filter events that are then processed by a software-based
high-level trigger. This further reduces the average recorded collision rate to
approximately
1\text{\,}\mathrm{kHz}
.

3
Data and simulation samples

The data used in this analysis were taken from
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions at the LHC where
proton bunches are collided every
25\text{\,}\mathrm{ns}
at
\sqrt{s}=$13\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV}$
.
A combination of several triggers for single light leptons, two light leptons and two hadronically decaying
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-leptons were used
to record the data for the analysis, depending on the analysis channel (see
Section

5.1
).
After data quality requirements, the samples used for this measurement
consist of
3.2\text{\,}{\mathrm{fb}}^{-1}
of data recorded in 2015, with an average of 14 interactions per
bunch crossing, and
32.9\text{\,}{\mathrm{fb}}^{-1}
recorded in 2016, with an average of 25 interactions
per bunch crossing.

Samples of signal and background processes were simulated using various MC generators as summarized in
Table

1
.
The signal contributions considered include the following four processes for Higgs-boson production at the LHC:
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
,
VBF
and associated production of a Higgs boson with a vector boson (
V\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
) or with a top–antitop quark pair (
\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{t}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
)
where all decay modes for the
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
process are included.
Other Higgs production processes such as associated production with a bottom–antibottom quark pair and with a single top quark are found to be negligible.
Higgs decays into
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
are considered background and simulated similarly for these production processes.
The mass of the Higgs boson was assumed to be
125\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}

[
31
]
.

corresponding PDF set and the model of parton showering, hadronization and underlying event (UEPS).
In addition, the order of the total cross-section calculation is given.
The total cross section for
VBF
production is calculated at approximate-NNLO QCD. More details are given in the text.

NLO

Higgs production by
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
was simulated with the
Powheg-Box
v2
[
32
,
33
,
34
,
35
]
NNLOPS program
[
36
]
at next-to-leading-order (NLO) accuracy in quantum chromodynamics (QCD) using the MiNLO approach
[
37
]
, and reweighted to next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO) in QCD in the Higgs rapidity.
The
VBF
and
V\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production processes were simulated at NLO accuracy in QCD using
Powheg-Box
with the MiNLO approach.
The
\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{t}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production process was simulated with
Madgraph5
_aMC@NLO
v2.2.2
[
38
]
at NLO accuracy in QCD.
For these signal samples, the simulation was interfaced to the
\textsc{Pythia}\ 8.212

[
39
]
model of parton showering, hadronization and underlying event (UEPS).
To estimate the impact of UEPS uncertainties, the
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
,
VBF
and
V\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
samples were also simulated with the
Herwig 7.0.3

[
40
,
41
]
UEPS model.
The
PDF4LHC15

[
42
]
parameterization of the parton
distribution functions (PDFs) was used for these production processes.
The

AZNLO

[
43
]
set of tuned parameters was used, with the

CTEQ6L1

[
44
]
PDF set, for the modeling of
non-perturbative effects.
For the
\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{t}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production process the
NNPDF30LO

[
45
]
PDF parametrization was used in the matrix element and the
NNPDF23LO

[
46
]
PDF parametrization for the UEPS model with the
A14

[
47
]
set of tuned parameters for the modeling of non-perturbative effects.

Photos++
version 3.52
[
48
]
was used for QED emissions from electroweak (EW) vertices and charged leptons.

The overall normalization of the
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
process is taken from a
next-to-next-to-next-to-leading-order (N
3
LO)
QCD calculation with NLO EW corrections
included
[
49
,
50
,
51
,
52
]
.
Production by
VBF
is normalized to an approximate-NNLO QCD cross
section with NLO EW corrections included
[
53
,
54
,
55
]
.
The
V\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
samples are normalized to cross sections calculated at NNLO in QCD,
with NLO EW corrections included
[
56
,
57
,
58
]
.
The
\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{t}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
process is normalized to a cross section calculated at NLO in QCD with
NLO EW corrections applied
[
59
,
60
,
61
,
62
,
63
,
64
]
.

Background samples of EW production of
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
bosons from
VBF
,
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-boson production with associated jets and diboson production processes were simulated
with the
Sherpa
2.2.1
[
65
]
generator.
Matrix elements were calculated using the Comix
[
66
]
and
OpenLoops
[
67
]
matrix-element generators and merged
with the
Sherpa
UEPS model
[
68
]
using the

ME+PS@NLO
prescription
[
69
]
.
For
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production with associated jets the matrix elements were calculated for up to two partons at NLO and four partons at LO precision.
Their inclusive cross sections are normalized to NNLO calculations from
Fewz

[
70
,
71
]
.
In particular, the dominant
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background is estimated using these simulations of
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-boson production.
For diboson production, the matrix elements were calculated for up to one additional parton at NLO and up to three additional partons at LO precision.
For all samples the
NNPDF30NNLO

[
45
]
PDF set was used together with
the
Sherpa
UEPS model.

The impact of UEPS uncertainties, and other modeling uncertainties such as LO/NLO precision comparison for leading jets, on the main background from
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
is studied in
an alternative sample which was simulated using
Madgraph5
_aMC@NLO
2.2.2
[
38
]
at leading order interfaced to the
\textsc{Pythia}\ 8.186
UEPS model. The A14 set of tuned parameters
[
47
]
was used together with the
NNPDF23LO
PDF set
[
46
]
.

For the generation of
\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{t}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production, the
Powheg-Box
v2
[
32
,
33
,
34
,
72
]

generator with the
CT10
PDF sets in the matrix element
calculations was used.
The predicted
\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{t}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
cross section was calculated with the
Top++
2.0 program to NNLO in perturbative QCD, including soft-gluon resummation to next-to-next-to-leading-log order
[
73
]
.
Single top-quark production of
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
was simulated
using the
Powheg-Box
v1
[
74
,
75
]
generator.
This generator uses the four-flavor scheme for the NLO matrix-element calculations together
with the fixed four-flavor PDF set
CT10F4
.
For all top-quark production processes, top-quark spin correlations
were preserved, using
MadSpin
[
76
]
for the t-channel.
The parton shower, hadronization, and the
underlying event were simulated using
\textsc{Pythia}\ 6.428

[
77
]
with the
CTEQ6L1
PDF set and the
corresponding Perugia 2012 set of tuned parameters
[
78
]
.
The top mass was assumed to be
172.5\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}
.
The EvtGen v.1.2.0 program
[
79
]
was used for the properties of
\mathit{{b}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
- and
\mathit{{c}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-hadron decays.

For all samples, a full simulation of the ATLAS detector
response
[
80
]
using the
Geant4

program
[
81
]
was performed.
The effect of multiple
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
interactions in the same and neighboring bunch
crossings (pileup) was included by overlaying minimum-bias events simulated
with
\textsc{Pythia}\ 8.186
using the MSTW2008LO PDF
[
82
]

and the A2
[
83
]
set of tuned parameters
on each generated signal and background event.
The number of overlaid events was chosen such that the distribution of the average
number of interactions per
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
bunch crossing in the simulation matches that
observed in data.

4
Object reconstruction

Electron candidates are reconstructed from energy deposits in the
electromagnetic calorimeter associated with a charged-particle track
measured in the inner detector. The electron candidates
are required to pass the

?
loose
?
likelihood-based identification
selection of Refs.
[
84
,
85
]
,
to have transverse momentum

p_{\text{T}}>$15\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
and to be in the fiducial volume of the
inner detector,
|\eta|<2.47
. The transition region between the barrel
and endcap calorimeters (
1.37<|\eta|<1.52
) is excluded.
The trigger efficiency for single electrons selected in the analysis ranges between 90% and 95%
[
86
]
.
Electron candidates are ignored if they share their reconstructed track with a muon candidate defined below
or if their angular distance from a jet is within
0.2<\Delta R<0.4
.

Muon candidates are constructed by matching an inner detector track
with a track reconstructed in the muon spectrometer
[
87
]
.
The muon candidates are required to have
p_{\text{T}}>$10\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$

and
|\eta|<2.5
and to pass the

?
loose
?
muon identification requirements of Ref.
[
87
]
.
The trigger efficiency for single muons selected in the analysis is close to 80% (70%) in the barrel in the 2016 (2015) dataset and 90% in the endcaps
[
86
]
.
Muon candidates are ignored if their angular distance from a jet is
\Delta R<0.4
with the following exceptions:
If
\Delta R<0.2
or the muon track is associated with the jet, and if the jet has either
less than three tracks or less than twice the transverse momentum of the
muon candidate, the jet is removed instead.
This recovers efficiency for muons that radiate a hard bremsstrahlung photon in the calorimeter.

In the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
signal regions, events are selected only if the selected electron and muon candidates satisfy their respective

?
medium
?
identification criteria.
The reconstruction and identification efficiency for muons with the

?
medium
?
identification requirement has been measured in
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events
[
87
]
. It is well above 98% over the full phase space, except for
|\eta|<0.1
where the reconstruction efficiency is about 70%.
The combined identification and reconstruction efficiency for

?
medium
?
electrons ranges from 80% to 90% in the
p_{\text{T}}
range of
10\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}80\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}
as measured in
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{e}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{e}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events
[
85
]
.
In addition, the electrons and muons must satisfy the

?
gradient
?
isolation criterion, which requires that there are no additional high-
p_{\text{T}}
tracks in a cone around the track and no significant energy deposits in a cone around the calorimeter clusters of the object after correcting for pileup. The size of the respective cones depends on the
p_{\text{T}}
of the light lepton.
This isolation requirement rejects about 10% of light leptons for low
p_{\text{T}}
and less than 1% for
p_{\text{T}}>$60\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$

[
87
,
85
]
.

Jets are reconstructed from topological clusters in the calorimeter using
the anti-
k_{t}
algorithm
[
88
,
89
]
, with a radius parameter
value
R=0.4
, and have
p_{\text{T}}>$20\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
and
|\eta|<4.9
.
To reject jets from pileup a

?
Jet Vertex Tagger
?
(JVT)
[
90
]

algorithm is used for jets with
p_{\text{T}}<$50\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
and
|\eta|<2.4
. It
employs a multivariate technique that relies on jet-tracking and calorimeter-cluster-shape variables
to determine the likelihood that the jet originates from pileup.
Similarly, pileup jets in the forward region are suppressed with a

?
forward JVT
?

[
91
]

algorithm, relying in this case only on calorimeter-cluster-shape variables, which is applied to all jets with
p_{\text{T}}<$50\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
and
|\eta|>2.5
.
In the pseudorapidity range
|\eta|<2.5
,
\mathit{{b}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-jets are selected
using a multivariate algorithm
[
92
,
93
]
.
A working point is chosen that corresponds to an efficiency of approximately 85% for
\mathit{{b}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-jets and rejection factors of 2.8 and 28 for
\mathit{{c}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-jets and light-flavor jets, respectively, in simulated
\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{t}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events.
A jet is ignored if it is within
\Delta R=0.2
of an electron or hadronically decaying
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidate.

Leptonic
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decays are reconstructed as electrons and muons. The reconstruction of the visible decay products of hadronic
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decays (
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
)
[
94
]
starts with a reconstructed jet
that has
p_{\text{T}}>$10\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
and
|\eta|<2.5
.
As in the case of electron reconstruction the transition region between the barrel and endcap calorimeters is excluded.
To discriminate
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
from jets initiated by light-quarks or gluons, an identification algorithm using multivariate techniques is applied to
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidates.
They have to pass the

?
loose
?
identification requirement of Ref.
[
94
]
.
In addition, the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidates are required to have
p_{\text{T}}>$20\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
, to have one or three associated tracks and an absolute electric charge of one.
Their energy is reconstructed by multivariate regression techniques using information about the associated tracks and calorimeter clusters, as well as the average number of collisions recorded.
The trigger efficiency per
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
selected in the analysis is 95% and 85% for 1-prong and 3-prong
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-leptons, respectively
[
95
]
.
The
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidates are ignored if they are within
\Delta R=0.2
of a muon or electron candidate
or if they have a high likelihood score of being an electron
[
85
]
.
The requirement on the likelihood score corresponds to a
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
efficiency measured in
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decays of 95%
[
94
]
.

In the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
signal regions, events are selected only if the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidate passes the

?
medium
?
identification requirement, corresponding to an efficiency of 55% and 40% for real 1-prong and 3-prong
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, respectively
[
94
]
. In addition,
if a 1-prong
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidate and an electron candidate are selected, a dedicated multivariate algorithm to reject electrons misidentified as
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
is applied to suppress
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{e}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{e}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events.
In the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
signal regions, both selected
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidates have to fulfill the

?
tight
?
identification requirement, which corresponds to a selection efficiency of 45% for real 1-prong
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and 30% for real 3-prong
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

[
94
]
.

The missing transverse momentum vector is calculated as the
negative vectorial sum of the
p_{\text{T}}
of the fully calibrated and
reconstructed physics objects
[
96
]
.
This procedure includes a soft
term, which is calculated from the inner detector tracks that originate
from the vertex associated with the hard-scattering process and that are not associated with any
of the reconstructed objects.
The missing transverse momentum (
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
) is defined as the magnitude of this vector.

The Higgs-boson candidate is reconstructed from the visible decay products of the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-leptons
and from the
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
, which is assumed to originate from the final-state neutrinos.
The di-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
invariant mass (
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
) is determined using the missing-mass calculator (MMC)
[
97
]
.
The standard deviation of the reconstructed di-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mass is
17.0\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}
,
15.3\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}
and
14.7\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}
for signal events selected in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
,
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channels, respectively.
The
p_{\text{T}}
of the Higgs-boson candidate (
p_{\text{T}}^{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
) is computed as the vector sum of the transverse momenta
of the visible decay products of the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-leptons and the missing transverse momentum vector.

5
Event selection and categorization

In addition to data quality criteria that ensure that the detector was functioning
properly, events are rejected if they contain reconstructed jets
associated with
energy deposits that can arise from hardware problems, beam-halo events
or cosmic-ray showers. Furthermore, events are required to have at least one reconstructed primary vertex with at least
two associated tracks with
p_{\text{T}}>$0.5\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
, which rejects non-collision events originating from cosmic rays or beam-halo
events.
The primary vertex is chosen as the
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
vertex candidate with the highest sum of the squared transverse momenta of all associated tracks.

The triggers and event selection for the three analysis channels are described in
Section

5.1
.
Selected events are categorized into exclusive signal regions, with enhanced signal-to-background
ratios. In addition, control regions are defined where a specific background is dominant, and thus a CR
facilitates the adjustment of the simulated prediction of a background contribution to match the observed data.
The signal and control regions are included in the fit described in
Section

8
.
They are described in
Section

5.2
together with validation regions (VRs) used to validate the simulation of the dominant
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\text{\,+\,jets}
background.

5.1
Event selection

Depending on the trigger, transverse momentum requirements are applied to selected electron, muon, and

\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidates.
They are summarized in
Table

2
and their per-object efficiencies are given in
Section

4
.
Due to the increasing luminosity and the different
pileup conditions, the
p_{\text{T}}
thresholds of the triggers were increased during
data-taking in 2016, which is taken into account in the
p_{\text{T}}
requirements of the event selection.
In the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel, the triggers for multiple light leptons are used only if the
highest-
p_{\text{T}}
light lepton does not pass the corresponding single-light-lepton trigger

p_{\text{T}}
requirement. This ensures that each trigger selects an exclusive set of events.

Table 2:
Summary of the triggers used to select events for the
three analysis channels during 2015 and 2016
data-taking and the corresponding
p_{\text{T}}
requirements applied in the
analysis.
For the electron+muon trigger the first number corresponds to the electron
p_{\text{T}}
requirement, the
second to the muon
p_{\text{T}}
requirement.
For the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel, at least one high-
p_{\text{T}}
jet in addition to the two
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidates is required for the 2016 dataset (see
Section

5.1
).

All channels require the exact number of identified

?
loose
?

leptons, i.e. electrons, muons and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, as defined in
Section

4
, corresponding to their respective final state.
Events with additional

?
loose
?
leptons are rejected.
The two leptons are required to be of opposite charge and they have to fulfill the
p_{\text{T}}
requirements of the respective trigger shown in
Table

2
.
The selected
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel is required to have
p_{\text{T}}>$30\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
.

The event selection for the three analysis channels is summarized in
Table

3
.
Only events with
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}>$20\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
are selected to reject events without neutrinos.
In the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel with two same-flavor (SF) light leptons this requirement is further tightened to suppress the large
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background.
For the same reason, requirements are tightened on the invariant mass of two light leptons (
m_{\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
) and a requirement is introduced on the
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
calculated only from the physics objects without the soft track term (
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss, hard}}
).
Requirements on the angular distance between the visible decay products of the two selected
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-lepton decays (
\Delta R_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
) and their
pseudorapidity difference (
|\Delta\eta_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}|
) are applied in all channels to reject non-resonant background events.
Requirements are applied to the fractions of the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-lepton momenta carried by each visible decay product

x_{i}=p^{\text{vis}}_{i}/\left(p^{\text{vis}}_{i}+p^{\text{miss}}_{i}\right)
, where
p^{\text{vis}}_{i}
and
p^{\text{miss}}_{i}
are the visible and missing momenta of the
i
th
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
lepton, ordered in descending
p_{\text{T}}
, calculated in the collinear
approximation
[
98
]
, to suppress events with
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
that is incompatible with a di-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decay.
Low transverse mass (
m_{\text{T}}
), calculated from
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
and the momentum of the selected light lepton,
is required in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel to reject events with leptonic
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decays.
A requirement on the di-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mass calculated in the collinear approximation (
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{coll}}
) of
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{coll}}>m_{\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}-$25\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
is introduced in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel to suppress events from
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and to ensure orthogonality between this measurement and the measurement of
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\to\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}^{*}\to\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\nu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\nu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

[
99
]
, which has a similar final state.

All channels require at least one jet (
j_{1}
) with
p_{\text{T}}^{j_{1}}>$40\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
to select Higgs bosons produced by VBF and to suppress background from
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events when selecting Higgs bosons produced through
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
.
Since 2016 the di-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
first-level trigger requires a jet with
p_{\text{T}}>$25\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
calibrated at trigger level with
|\eta|<3.2
in addition to the two
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidates.
In the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel the jet
p_{\text{T}}
requirement is thus raised to
p_{\text{T}}^{j_{1}}>$70\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
to achieve uniform trigger selection efficiency as a function of
p_{\text{T}}^{j_{1}}
. The trigger efficiency for the additional jet ranges from 95% to 100% for these requirements.
In the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channels, the top-quark background is suppressed by requiring that no jet with
p_{\text{T}}>$25\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
is tagged as a
\mathit{{b}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-jet.

5.2
Signal, control and validation regions

To exploit signal-sensitive event topologies, a

?
VBF

?
and a

?
boosted
?
analysis category are defined without any overlap in phase space.
The
VBF
category targets events with a Higgs boson produced by
VBF
and is characterized by the presence of a second high-
p_{\text{T}}
jet (
p_{\text{T}}^{j_{2}}>$30\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
).
In addition, the two jets are required to be in opposite hemispheres of the detector with a large pseudorapidity separation of
|\Delta\eta_{jj}|>3
and their invariant mass (
m_{jj}
) is required to be larger than
400\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}
.
The selected leptons are required to have
\eta
-values that lie between those of the two jets (

?
central leptons
?
).
Although this category is dominated by
VBF
production, it also includes significant contributions from
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
production, amounting to up to 30% of the total expected Higgs-boson signal.

The boosted category targets events with Higgs bosons produced through
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
with additional recoiling jets, which is motivated by the harder
p_{\text{T}}
-spectrum of the
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
signal compared to the dominant background from
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
.
It contains all events with
p_{\text{T}}^{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}>$100\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
that do not pass the
VBF
selection.
In addition to events from
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
, the boosted categories contain sizable contributions from
VBF
and
V\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production of 10–20% of the expected signal.
Events that pass the event selection, detailed in
Table

3
, but do not fall into the
VBF
or boosted categories, are not used in the analysis.

Using
p_{\text{T}}^{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
,
\Delta R_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
and
m_{jj}
, the
VBF
and boosted categories, referred to as

?
inclusive
?
categories, are split further into 13 exclusive signal regions with different signal-to-background ratios to improve the sensitivity.

Table

4
summarizes the analysis categories and signal region definitions.

illustrates the expected signal and background composition in the signal and control regions of all analysis channels.

, for

in the boosted inclusive categories, and for
m_{jj}
in the
VBF
inclusive categories.
The observed data agree within the given uncertainties with the background expectation described in
Section

6
for all distributions.

Six control regions are defined to constrain the normalization of the dominant backgrounds in regions of phase space where their purity is high.
Their definitions are summarized in
Table

5
.
Two
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
CRs, which are both more than 90% pure in
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events, are defined by applying the same selection as for the
SF
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

VBF
and boosted inclusive regions, respectively, but with the
m_{\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
requirement modified to
80<m_{\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}<$100\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
.
The top-quark background is characterized by the presence of
\mathit{{b}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-jets.
Four separate top CRs are defined by inverting the
\mathit{{b}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-jet veto in the inclusive
VBF
and boosted categories for each of the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channels.
The top CRs in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel are about 80% pure in top-quark events.
For the top CRs in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel, the requirement of
m_{\text{T}}<$70\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
is replaced by
m_{\text{T}}>$40\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
to further enhance the purity to about 70% in the
VBF
top CR and about 60% in the boosted top CR.
No such control regions are defined for the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel since the top and
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
backgrounds are negligible in this case.

One validation region is defined for each signal region (

?
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
VRs
?
) to validate
the event yields and kinematic distributions of simulated
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events.
The
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
VRs are composed of
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events with kinematics similar to the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background in the respective signal regions.
This is achieved by starting with an event selection that is
based on the SF
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel preselection with
the following differences that account for the
selection of light leptons instead of decay products from
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-leptons:
The
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{coll}}
,
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
and
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss, hard}}
requirements are dropped and the
m_{\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
requirement is inverted to

m_{\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}>$80\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
.
The other requirements on
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-lepton decays are replaced with requirements on the two light leptons.
In particular, the requirements on
p_{\text{T}}^{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
are substituted by the
p_{\text{T}}
of the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
boson computed from the
p_{\text{T}}
of the light leptons (
p_{\text{T}}^{\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
).
Requirements on jets are unchanged since they define the shape of most kinematic distributions for
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-boson production similarly in the SRs and the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
VRs.
More than 99% of the selected events are from
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
in all
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
VRs.

6
Background estimation

The final-state topologies of the three analysis channels have different background
compositions, which necessitates different strategies for the background estimation. In each SR,
the expected number of background events and the associated kinematic distributions are
derived from a mixture of data-driven methods and simulation.

Background contributions with
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, with prompt light leptons and with light leptons
from
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-lepton decays are estimated from simulation.
If their contribution is significant, their normalization is constrained by the observed event yields in CRs.
For smaller contributions of this type, their normalization is entirely taken from the
theoretical cross sections with the precision in QCD listed in
Table

1
.
This includes di-boson processes and a small contribution from EW production of
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
/
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
bosons from
VBF
.
Contributions from light- and heavy-flavor jets that are misidentified as
prompt, light leptons or
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
are estimated using data-driven methods.
They are labeled as

?
fake-
\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
?
and

?
fake-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
?
backgrounds, respectively,
and collectively as

?
misidentified
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
?
, throughout this paper.
The contamination from
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\to\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}^{*}
decays is treated
as a background in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel, while it is negligible in other channels.

For the background sources that have
their normalization constrained using data,
Table

6
shows the
normalization factors and their uncertainties obtained from the fit (see
Section

8
).
For simulated backgrounds, the factors compare the background
normalizations with
values determined from their theoretical cross sections. The
normalization factor for the data-driven fake-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background
scales the event yield of the template of events that fail the
opposite-charge requirement (see
Section

6.4
).
The
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
normalization is constrained by data in the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distributions of the signal regions.
Systematic uncertainties are the dominant contribution to the normalization factor uncertainties.

normalization constrained using data

but without uncertainties in total simulated cross sections extrapolated to the selected phase space.

6.1
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background validation

The Drell–Yan process
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\to\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/\mathit{{\gamma}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{*}}}\to\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
is a dominant irreducible background in
all analysis categories and contributes between 50% and 90% of the total background depending
on the signal region. The separation between the Drell–Yan and the
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
signal processes
is limited by the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
resolution.

The modeling of this important background is validated using
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
VRs that consist of
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events.
In
Figure

3
, the observed distributions of several variables
are compared with simulation normalized to the event yield in data.
The selected observables correspond to either variables correlated with
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
(
p_{\text{T}}^{\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}_{1}}
and
p_{\text{T}}^{\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}_{2}}
), or to major variables used for categorization (
p_{\text{T}}^{\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
,
\Delta R_{\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
,
\Delta\eta_{jj}
and
m_{jj}
), or to variables to which different requirements are applied in each decay channel (
p_{\text{T}}^{j_{1}}
).
Generally, the
Sherpa
simulation describes the shape of data distributions within the experimental and theoretical uncertainties (see
Section

7
),
with the exception of a slight trend in the ratio of data to simulation as a function of
\Delta\eta_{jj}
and
m_{jj}
shown in
Figure

3
.
These trends have no impact on the modeling of
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
.
Reweighting the simulation with the observed
m_{jj}
distribution, which is an important variable for
VBF
categorization, has a negligible impact on the measurement.
In the fit, the normalization of the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background is correlated across the decay channels and
constrained by data in the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distributions of the signal regions associated with the boosted
and
VBF
categories, independently.
As shown in
Table

6
, it is constrained to
\pm
5% in the boosted category
and to
\pm
9% in the
VBF
category.
The relative acceptance of events among the signal regions within a category is validated by applying the corresponding event-selection criteria to the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
VRs.
The expected relative acceptance from simulation agrees with data within uncertainties for all regions.

Figures

8
and
9
show the good modeling of the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distribution in all signal regions.
Additional uncertainties in the relative acceptances
and in the shape of the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distributions in the signal regions
are evaluated from theoretical and experimental uncertainties
described in

.

6.2
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

Decays of
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
bosons into light leptons are a significant background for
the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

of light-lepton pairs towards values similar to those expected for the signal.

\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel to
\pm
40% in the
VBF
category
and to
\pm
25% in the boosted category, as shown in
Table

6
.
The good modeling of the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distribution in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

6.3
Top-quark background

The production of
\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{t}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
pairs or single top quarks is a significant background (

?
top background
?
)
for the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channels, due to the production of prompt light leptons with
associated
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
in the top-quark decay chain
\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\to\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{b}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}},\,\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\to\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\nu,\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\nu
.
Events where a selected
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-lepton decay product is misidentified, are estimated using data-driven methods that are
discussed in
Section

6.4
. The remaining top background is estimated from simulation.
In the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channels the normalization of simulated top background is additionally constrained by the
absolute event yields in their respective top CRs to
\pm
30% in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

VBF
top CR
and less than
\pm
10% in the other top CRs, as shown in
Table

6
.
Figures
4
(b) and
4
