m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distributions in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
boosted top CR and the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

VBF
top CR, respectively.

6.4
Backgrounds from misidentified
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

Apart from the small contribution from light leptons misidentified as

\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
described in
Section

6.2
, hadronic jets can be misidentified
as
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, electrons and muons.
These sources of background contribute up to half of the total background, depending
on the signal region, and are estimated with
data-driven techniques. Since the background sources depend on the event
topology, specific methods are applied to each individual channel.

In the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel, the
main sources of the fake-
\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background are multijets,
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
bosons in association with jets, and semileptonically decaying
\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{t}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events.
All these background sources are treated together.
Fake-
\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
regions are defined in data by requiring that the light lepton with the second-highest
p_{\text{T}}
does not satisfy
the

?
gradient
?
isolation criterion.
This is referred to as

?
inverted
?
isolation. In addition, if the light lepton is an electron, its identification criteria
are relaxed to

?
loose
?
.
Fake-
\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
templates are
created from these samples by subtracting top and
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
backgrounds that produce real light leptons, estimated from simulation. The
normalization of each template is then scaled by a factor that
corrects for the inverted-isolation requirement.
These correction factors are computed for each combination of lepton flavor
from events that pass the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
selection but have same-charge light leptons,
subtracting simulated top and
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
backgrounds.
Fake-
\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background in the top-quark CRs is estimated following the same procedure.

Systematic uncertainties in the shape and
normalization of the fake-
\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel
depend on the
p_{\text{T}}
of the second-highest-
p_{\text{T}}
lepton and are estimated as follows.
A closure test of the background estimate is
performed using events where the leptons are required to have the same charge
and yields an uncertainty ranging between 20% and 65%. An uncertainty in the
heavy-flavor content is estimated by using isolation correction factors
that are computed from samples selected with inverted
\mathit{{b}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-jet requirements.
This uncertainty is as large as 50%.
Minor contributions come from the uncertainty in the fractional
composition of the fake-
\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background in top-quark decays, multijet
events and
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-boson production.

In the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel, a

?
fake-factor
?
method is used to derive
estimates for fake-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events,
composed mainly of multijet events and

\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-boson production in association with jets.
A fake-factor is defined as the ratio of the number of events where the highest-
p_{\text{T}}
jet is identified
as a

?
medium
?

\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidate to the number of events with a highest-
p_{\text{T}}
jet that passes
a very loose
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
identification but fails the

?
medium
?
one.
Fake-factors
depend on the
p_{\text{T}}
and track multiplicity of the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidate and
on the type of parton initiating the jet.
Therefore, they are computed depending on the
p_{\text{T}}
and the track multiplicity,
in both quark-jet-dominated

?
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-enhanced
?
and gluon-jet-dominated

?
multijet-enhanced
?
regions.
The
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-enhanced regions are defined by inverting the
m_{\text{T}}<$70\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
requirement and the multijet-enhanced regions are defined
by inverting the light-lepton isolation, relative to the inclusive boosted and
VBF
selections.
Backgrounds from
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-boson production with associated jets and semileptonically decaying
\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{t}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
have fake-factors similar to those
found in backgrounds from
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
bosons, and their contributions are negligible.
The fake-factors are in the range 0.15–0.25 for 1-prong and 0.01–0.04 for 3-prong
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
.
To obtain the fake-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background estimate for the signal regions, these fake-factors are first weighted by the multijets-to-
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
fraction.
The weighted fake-factors are then applied to events in regions
defined by the selections of the corresponding signal regions, except that the highest-
p_{\text{T}}

\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidate passes a very loose
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
identification and fails the

?
medium
?
one (

?
anti-ID
?
regions).
The relative multijet contribution in each anti-ID region is estimated from the yield of events that fail the light-lepton isolation requirement,
multiplied by a factor that corrects for this requirement.
The multijet contribution varies by more than 50% and
depends on the lepton
p_{\text{T}}
and on the
\Delta\phi
between
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
.
The good agreement between data and background estimates is shown in
Figure

5
(a)
for the main discriminant of the analysis,
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
, in the boosted
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-enhanced region.

The dominant contribution to the uncertainties in the fake-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background
in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel originates from the
statistical uncertainty in the individual fake-factors of up to
10% in the boosted signal regions and up to 35% in the VBF
signal regions. Minor contributions originate from the statistical
uncertainty in the anti-ID regions and uncertainties in the fractional
size of the multijet contribution to the fake-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background.

((b))

-enhanced region of the

-axis range are shown in the last bin of each distribution.

In the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel, the multijet background is modeled using a template extracted
from data that pass the signal-region selections, but where the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidates
are allowed to have two tracks and required to fail the opposite-charge requirement
(nOC region).
The contribution of events with true
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-leptons from other SM processes is subtracted
from this template using simulation. The template is then reweighted using scale factors
dependent on the difference in
\phi
between the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidates (
\Delta\phi_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
).
These scale factors are derived
by comparing the template from an nOC selection with a region
obtained by requiring the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
pair to have opposite charge and the second-highest-
p_{\text{T}}

\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
to fail the

?
tight
?
but pass the

?
medium
?
identification requirements.
As the yield of events that pass these identification requirements is small, the scale factors are derived from events
that pass the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
selection with looser
\Delta\eta_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
and
\Delta R_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
requirements to gain statistical power.
The normalization of the multijet background is constrained in the fit by data in the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distribution
in the signal regions.
For this, a normalization factor is defined and it is correlated across all
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
signal regions.

Figure

5
(b) shows good agreement between data and background
predictions in the distribution of
\Delta\eta
between the
two
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, which has a quite different shape for the multijets than for the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
process.
In this figure, events are selected that pass the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
boosted low-
p_{\text{T}}^{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
selection.
Contributions from other backgrounds, such as
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
with associated jets, range from 2% to 5% in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
SRs.

The event yield of the multijet background in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel is
constrained by data to
\pm
15% in the signal regions as shown in
Table

6
.
The dominant contribution to the uncertainties that affect the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
shape
originates from the statistical uncertainties in the

\Delta\phi_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
scale factors
and amounts to 8%. The systematic
uncertainty in these scale factors is
estimated by comparing them with scale factors computed from the nOC
region and a CR defined by requiring opposite-charge
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
to pass

?
loose
?
but not

?
medium
?
identification. Minor contributions arise from
the uncertainty in the extrapolation from the nOC requirement and the uncertainty
from the subtraction of simulated backgrounds. The combination of these uncertainties
leads to a total variation in the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
template shape by at most 10% between bins.

7
Systematic uncertainties

The expected signal and background yields in the various signal and control regions
as well as the shape of the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distributions in the signal regions are affected by systematic uncertainties.
These are discussed below, grouped into three categories: theoretical uncertainties in signal, theoretical uncertainties in background, and experimental uncertainties.
The uncertainties in backgrounds from misidentified
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-leptons, which are estimated using data-driven techniques, are discussed in
Section

6.4
.
The effects of all uncertainties are included in the fit model described in
Section

8
.

7.1
Theoretical uncertainties in signal

The procedures to estimate the uncertainty in the Higgs production cross sections
follow the recommendations by the LHC Higgs Cross Section Working
Group
[
100
]
.
They are briefly summarized below.
Uncertainties are evaluated separately for their impact on the total cross section,
their impact on the acceptance in different SRs, and on the shape of the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distribution in each SR.

The cross section of
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
production in association with an exclusive number of additional jets has large uncertainties from higher-order QCD corrections
[
101
]
.
In this analysis, the boosted and
VBF
categories almost exclusively select
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
events with one and two additional jets, respectively.
To take this effect into account, nine uncertainty sources are included.
Four sources account for uncertainties in the jet multiplicities due to missing higher-order corrections: Two sources account for yield uncertainties and two sources account for migration uncertainties of zero to one jets and one to at least two jets in the event, respectively, using the STWZ
[
102
]
and BLPTW
[
102
,
103
,
104
]
predictions as an input.
Three uncertainty sources parameterize modeling uncertainties in the Higgs-boson
p_{\text{T}}
, two of which encapsulate the migration uncertainty between the intermediate and high-
p_{\text{T}}
regions of events with at least one jet, and one which encapsulates the treatment of the top-quark mass in the loop corrections, where the difference between the LO and NLO predictions is taken as an uncertainty due to missing higher-order corrections.
Two sources account for the acceptance uncertainties of
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
production in the
VBF
phase space from selecting exactly two and at least three jets, respectively. Their size is estimated using an extension of the Stewart–Tackmann method
[
105
,
106
]
.
The resulting acceptance uncertainties from these nine sources range from 1% to 10%, with the dominant uncertainties due to the modeling of the Higgs
p_{\text{T}}
distribution in all SRs, to the scale variations in the boosted SRs, and to the acceptance uncertainties in the
VBF
signal regions.

For
VBF
and
V\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production cross sections, the
uncertainties due to missing higher-order QCD corrections are
estimated by varying the factorization and renormalization scales by
factors of two around the nominal scale.
The resulting uncertainties in the total cross section are below 1% for
VBF
and
\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

production and below 5% for
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production.
The uncertainties in the acceptance in the different SRs are about
1% for
VBF
production in all categories.
For
V\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production the relative acceptance uncertainty ranges between
-10\%
and
+20\%

in
VBF
SRs. It is below 10% in boosted SRs.

Uncertainties related to the simulation of the underlying event, hadronization and parton shower for all signal samples are estimated by comparing the acceptance when using the default UEPS model from
\textsc{Pythia}\ 8.212
with an alternative UEPS model from
Herwig 7.0.3
.
The resulting acceptance uncertainties range from 2% to 26% for
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
production and from 2% to 18% for
VBF
production, depending on the signal region.
The PDF uncertainties are estimated using 30 eigenvector variations and two
\alpha_{\text{S}}
variations
that are evaluated independently relative to the default PDF set
PDF4LHC15

[
42
]
.
The total uncertainty due to these variations is 5% or less depending on the SR and the Higgs production mode.
Finally, an uncertainty in the
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decay branching ratio
of 1%
[
100
]
affects the signal rates.
All sources of theoretical uncertainties in the signal expectation are correlated across SRs.

7.2
Theoretical uncertainties in backgrounds

Uncertainties from missing higher-order corrections, the PDF parameterization and the UEPS modelling are also considered for the dominant
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background.
The UEPS modelling uncertainties are estimated by comparing with an alternative
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
sample as described in
Section

3
.
Since its overall normalization is constrained separately in the
VBF
and boosted SRs, variations due to these uncertainties are considered in the event migration within an analysis channel, in the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
shape and in the relative change in acceptance between the three analysis channels.
These variations are treated as uncorrelated between the
VBF
and boosted SRs.
In addition, the first two types of variations are treated as uncorrelated between the three analysis channels.
This treatment accounts for the differences in the corresponding event selections.
The largest uncertainties are due to the CKKW matching
[
107
]
and are evaluated as a function of the number of true jets and the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-boson
p_{\text{T}}
.
They vary between 1% and 5% depending on the SR.
The uncertainty in the measured cross section for electroweak
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production with two associated jets
[
108
]
is found to be small compared to the other uncertainties in
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-boson production.

The top-quark background normalization in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channels as well as the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background normalization in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
channel are constrained by data in dedicated CRs.
All other simulated background contributions are normalized to their Monte Carlo prediction.
For all simulated background contributions, other than
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, no theoretical uncertainties are considered, as their impact is small compared to the uncertainties in the dominant backgrounds from
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and misidentified leptons.

7.3
Experimental uncertainties

Experimental systematic uncertainties result from uncertainties in
efficiencies for triggering, object reconstruction and identification,
as well as from uncertainties in the energy scale and resolution of
jets,
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, light leptons and
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
.
These uncertainties affect both the event
yields and the shape of the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
.
The dominant experimental
uncertainties in the final result are related to jet and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
reconstruction.
The impact of the electron- and muon-related
uncertainties
[
87
,
109
,
86
]

on the measurement are small.
Uncertainties in the integrated
luminosity affect the number of predicted signal and background events,
with the exception of processes that are normalized to data, see
Table

6
.
This uncertainty is 2.1% for the combined 2015+2016
dataset. It is derived using a methodology similar to that detailed in
Ref.
[
110
]
, and using the LUCID-2 detector for the baseline
luminosity measurements
[
111
]
, from a calibration of the luminosity
scale using
x
-
y
beam-separation scans.

The uncertainties of the

\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
identification efficiency are in the range of 2–4.5%
for the reconstruction efficiency
[
112
]
,
3–14% for the trigger efficiency (depending on the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

p_{\text{T}}
), 5–6% for the identification efficiency and 3–14%
for the rate at which an electron is misidentified as

\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
(depending on the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

\eta
)
[
94
]
.
The uncertainties of the
\mathit{{b}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-tagging efficiencies are measured in
dedicated calibration analyses
[
92
]
and are decomposed
into uncorrelated components.
Uncertainties in the efficiency to pass the JVT and forward JVT requirements are also considered
[
113
,
91
]
.
Simulated events are corrected for
differences in these efficiencies between data and simulation and the
associated uncertainties are propagated through the analysis.

The uncertainties of the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
energy
scale
[
94
]
are determined
by fitting the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-boson mass in
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events, reconstructed using the visible
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decay products.
The precision amounts to 2–3%, which is dominated by the
uncertainty of background modeling.
Additional uncertainties based on the modeling of the calorimeter response to single particles are added for
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
with
p_{\text{T}}>$50\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$

[
114
]
.
The jet energy scale and its uncertainty are derived by combining
information from test-beam data, LHC collision data and
simulation
[
115
]
. The uncertainties from these
measurements are factorized into eight
principal components.
Additional uncertainties that are considered are related to jet flavor, pileup
corrections,
\eta
-dependence, and high-
p_{\text{T}}
jets, yielding a total of
20 independent sources. The uncertainties amount to 1–6% per jet,
depending on the jet
p_{\text{T}}
. The jet energy resolution
uncertainties
[
116
]
are divided into 11
independent components and amount to 1–6%.

Since systematic uncertainties of the energy scales of all objects
affect the reconstructed
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
, this is recalculated after each
variation is applied. The scale uncertainty of
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
due to the energy
in the calorimeter cells not associated with physics objects is also
taken into account
[
96
]
. The uncertainty of the
resolution of
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
arises from the energy resolution uncertainties of
each of the
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
terms and the modeling of pileup and its effects on the soft term (see
Section

4
).

8
Results

Maximum-likelihood fits are performed on data to extract parameters of
interest that probe
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production with increasing granularity. Firstly, a
single parameter is fitted to measure the total cross section of the

\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production processes.
Then, a two-parameter cross-section fit is
presented separating the
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
and
VBF
production processes. Finally,
a three-parameter fit is performed to measure
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
production
cross sections in two exclusive regions of phase space.
For the small contribution from
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\to\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{W}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}^{*}
decays, the measurements assume
the SM predictions for production cross section and branching ratio.

A probability model is constructed that describes the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distributions in the 13 signal regions and the event yields in 6 control regions.
The latter are included to constrain the normalizations of the dominant backgrounds.
Each signal region is modeled by a product of Poisson distributions, where each such distribution describes the expected event count in intervals of
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
.
Each control region is modeled by a single Poisson distribution that
describes the total expected event count in that region.
Signal and background predictions depend on systematic uncertainties, which are parameterized as nuisance parameters and are constrained
using Gaussian or log-normal probability distributions.
The latter are used for normalization factors (see
Table

6
) to ensure that they are
always positive.
The dependence of the predictions on nuisance parameters related to systematic uncertainties is modeled with an interpolation
approach between yields obtained at different fixed systematic uncertainty settings.
A smoothing procedure is applied to remove occasional large local fluctuations in the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distribution templates, which encode
systematic uncertainties of some background processes in certain regions.
For the measurements,
all theoretical uncertainties are included, except those related to
the respective measured signal cross sections, and are correlated as described in
Section

7.1
.
The experimental uncertainties are fully correlated across categories and
the background modeling uncertainties are generally uncorrelated, with the exception of the
normalization factors as described in
Section

6
.
Estimates of the parameters of interest and the confidence intervals are calculated with
the profile likelihood ratio
[
117
]
test statistic, whereas the test statistic
\tilde{q}_{0}

[
117
]

is used to compute the significances of the deviations from the background-only hypothesis.

The observed (expected) significance of the signal excess relative to the background-only hypothesis computed from the
likelihood fit is 4.4 (4.1) standard deviations, compatible with a SM Higgs boson with a mass

m_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}=$125\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
.
This result is combined with the result of the search for
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
using data at 7 and
8\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV}
center-of-mass energies
[
15
]
.
The combined observed (expected) significance amounts to 6.4 (5.4) standard deviations.
In this combination, all nuisance parameters are treated as uncorrelated between the two analyses.
In particular, the dominant
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
background is estimated differently, as mentioned in
Section

1
.

The parameter
{\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}\equiv\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}\cdot\mathcal{B}(\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}})}
is
fitted, where
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
is the total cross section of the
considered Higgs-boson production processes
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
,
VBF
,
V\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{t}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\mkern 0.0mu\overline{\mkern 0.0mu{t}}}{}_{\mspace{-2mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{0mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, and
where
\mathcal{B}(\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}})
is the
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
branching fraction.
For this measurement, the relative contributions from the various Higgs production processes are assumed as predicted by the SM
and the uncertainties related to the predicted total signal cross section are excluded.
The measured value of
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
is

$3.76558372941$\,^{+$0.601597224288$}_{-$0.591224858352$}\,\text{(stat.)}\,^{+$0.874736193936$}_{-$0.74335289208$}\,\text{(syst.)}\,$\mathrm{pb}$
,
consistent with the SM prediction,
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{SM}}=$3.457455312$\pm$0.131919772196$\,$\mathrm{pb}$

[
100
]
.
The signal strength
\mu_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
is
defined as the ratio of the measured signal yield to the
Standard Model expectation. It is computed by the fit
described above, including uncertainties in the predicted signal
cross section, and is evaluated to be

1.09\,^{+0.18}_{-0.17}\,\text{(stat.)}\,^{+0.26}_{-0.22}\,\text{(syst.)}\,^{+0.16}_{-0.11}\,\text{(theory syst.)}
.

Tables

7
,
8
and
9
summarize the expected signal and background yields computed by the fit in each signal region for the
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
measurement.
The signal event yields are given separately for each production
process of relevance. Within the uncertainties, good agreement is observed
between the data and the predicted sum of signal and background contributions, for
a SM Higgs boson of mass
m_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}=$125\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
with the
measured value of
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
reported above.

Table

10
shows a summary of the dominant uncertainties in

\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
, grouped by their respective sources.
Figure

6
shows the systematic
uncertainties with the largest impact, together with a comparison with their
nominal values used as input to the fit.
In both the table and the figure the shown uncertainties are
ranked by their fractional impact on the measurement of
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
.
To compute the impact for each
nuisance parameter, a separate fit is performed again with the parameter fixed to its
fitted value, and the resulting uncertainty in
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
is subtracted from the uncertainty obtained in the original fit via variance subtraction.
The dominant uncertainties
are related to the limited number of events in the simulated samples,
the missing higher-order QCD corrections to the signal
process cross sections, the jet energy resolution, the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had-vis}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
identification and the normalizations of the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
backgrounds.

Figure

6
also shows that in most cases the fitted parameters are in agreement
with the nominal values,
except for the uncertainties related to jet
energy resolution and scale.
In the case of real di-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events, the distribution of
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
is sensitive to the jet-related uncertainties because
selected di-
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events in the
VBF
and boosted categories are characterized by one or more high-
p_{\text{T}}
jets that
recoil against the two
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-leptons.
The main contributions to
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
are thus the neutrinos in the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-lepton decays and the impact of the jet energy resolution when projected onto the
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
direction.
Applying both the jet energy resolution and scale uncertainties causes a shift in the mean jet
p_{\text{T}}
, which therefore translates directly into a shift of the reconstructed
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
.
This, in turn, translates into a shift of the reconstructed
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
that is constrained by data in the region of the
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mass peak.

Figure 6:
Fractional impact of systematic uncertainties in
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
as computed by
the fit. The systematic uncertainties are listed in decreasing
order of their impact on
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
on the
y
-axis. The hatched
blue and open blue boxes show the variations of
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}
referring to the
top
x
-axis (impact), as described in the text.
The filled circles, referring to the bottom
x
-axis,
show the pulls of the fitted nuisance parameters, i.e. the
deviations of the fitted parameters
\hat{\theta}
from their
nominal values
\theta_{0}
, normalized to their nominal
uncertainties
\Delta\theta
. The black lines show the
uncertainties of the nuisance parameters resulting from the fit.
Several sources of uncertainties such as the jet energy scale and resolution as well as the
\mathit{{b}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
-mistag rate are described by their principal components in the fit.
The open circles, also referring to the bottom
x

values of the fitted
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{Z}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\ell}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
normalization factors in the
boosted category as listed in
Table

6
.
Their uncertainties do not include uncertainties in total simulated cross sections extrapolated to the selected phase space.

Results of the fit when only the data of an individual channel or of an individual category are used,
are shown in
Figure

7
. Also shown is the result from the
fit and the uncertainty in
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{SM}}
.
All results are consistent with the SM expectations.
The simple combination of the individual fit results does not agree exactly with
the result of the combined fit because the values of the nuisance
parameters are different.
The
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distributions in all signal regions with background predictions adjusted by the likelihood fit are shown in
Figures

and

in the Appendix.
The
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distributions for the predicted signal plus background are compared with the data in
Figure

8
, separately for the combined signal regions of
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
,
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
analysis channels, and in
Figure

9
, separately for the combined
VBF
and the combined boosted signal regions.

distributions in all signal regions is shown in
Figure

.
The events are weighted by a factor of
\ln(1+S/B)
which enhances the events compatible with the signal hypothesis.

Figure

7
illustrates that the
VBF
and boosted categories provide good sensitivity, respectively, to
VBF
and
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
Higgs-boson production.
A two-parameter fit is therefore performed to determine the cross sections of these production processes by exploiting the sensitivity offered by the use of the event categories in the analysis of the three channels.
Two cross-section parameters
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{VBF}}
and
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}}
are introduced
and the data are fitted to these parameters, separating
the vector-boson-mediated

VBF
process from the fermion-mediated
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
process, while the contributions from other Higgs production
processes are set to their predicted SM values.
The two-dimensional
68\%
and
95\%
confidence level
(CL) contours in the plane of
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{VBF}}
and
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}}
are shown in
Figure

11
. The best-fit values are

\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{VBF}}=$0.2800453818$\pm$0.09$\,\text{(stat.)}\,^{+$0.106$}_{-$0.093$}\,\text{(syst.)}\,$\mathrm{pb}$

and

\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}}=$3.096016028$\pm$1.0$\,\text{(stat.)}\,^{+$1.63$}_{-$1.258$}\,\text{(syst.)}\,$\mathrm{pb}$
,
in agreement with the predictions from the Standard Model of

\sigma_{\text{VBF},\ \mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{SM}}=$0.23721$\pm 0.006\,$\mathrm{pb}$

and

\sigma_{\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F},\ \mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{SM}}=$3.0469$\pm$0.128994749633$\,$\mathrm{pb}$

[
100
]
. The two
results are strongly anti-correlated (correlation coefficient of

-52
%), as can be seen in
Figure

11
.

The
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
signal provides enough events to measure
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
cross
sections in mutually exclusive regions of the
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
phase space. Two

\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
regions are defined by particle-level events with at least one
jet where a jet is required to have
p_{\text{T}}>$30\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
: events with a
Higgs-boson
p_{\text{T}}
of
60<p_{\text{T}}^{H}<$120\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
and events with

p_{\text{T}}^{H}>$120\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
.
A cross-section parameter for each of the two
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
regions is
introduced, along with a parameter for
VBF
production in an inclusive
region, and a combined three-parameter fit is performed using the
event categories in the analysis of the three channels. The
particle-level definitions of all three phase-space regions closely follow
the framework of simplified template cross
sections
[
101
]
where the Higgs-boson rapidity
y_{H}

is required to satisfy
|y_{H}|<2.5
.
The
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
and
VBF
production cross sections outside the respective particle-level region requirements are set to the measured values reported above.
Cross sections of other Higgs-boson production processes are set to their SM values.

Table

11
shows the resulting cross sections along with the
SM predictions in the respective particle-level region.
The measurements in all regions have a precision similar to that of
the inclusive ggF and VBF measurements reported above.

for the combination of all channels in the
(

,

) plane. The 68% and 95% CL contours are shown as dashed
and solid lines, respectively, for
m_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}=$125\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$

in three mutually exclusive regions of phase space
of particle-level events.

in

.

\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
events that
fail the particle-level requirements of the two
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
regions is set to the measured
\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}}
value.
Results are shown along with
the SM predictions in the respective particle-level regions.
The definitions of the regions closely follow the framework of simplified
template cross sections
[
101
]
.

9
Conclusions

A measurement of total production cross sections of the Higgs boson
in proton–proton collisions is presented in the

\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decay channel. The analysis was performed using

36.1\text{\,}{\mathrm{fb}}^{-1}
of data recorded by the ATLAS experiment at the LHC
at a center-of-mass energy of
\sqrt{s}=$13\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV}$
.
All combinations of leptonic and hadronic
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decays were considered. An excess of events over the expected
background from other Standard Model processes was found with an
observed (expected) significance of 4.4 (4.1) standard deviations.
Combined with results using data taken
at
\sqrt{s}
of 7 and
8\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV}
, the observed (expected) significance amounts to 6.4 (5.4)
standard deviations and constitutes an observation of
\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decays by the ATLAS experiment.
Using the data taken at
\sqrt{s}=$13\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV}$
, the
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\to\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
total cross section
is measured to be

$3.76558372941$\,^{+$0.601597224288$}_{-$0.591224858352$}\,\text{(stat.)}\,^{+$0.874736193936$}_{-$0.74335289208$}\,\text{(syst.)}\,$\mathrm{pb}$
,
for a Higgs boson of mass
125\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}
.
A two-dimensional fit was performed to
separate the vector-boson-mediated
VBF
process from the
fermion-mediated
\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
process.
The cross sections of the Higgs boson decaying into two
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
leptons are measured to be

\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{VBF}}=$0.2800453818$\pm$0.09$\,\text{(stat.)}\,^{+$0.106$}_{-$0.093$}\,\text{(syst.)}\,$\mathrm{pb}$
and

\sigma_{\mathit{{H}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}}=$3.096016028$\pm$1.0$\,\text{(stat.)}\,^{+$1.63$}_{-$1.258$}\,\text{(syst.)}\,$\mathrm{pb}$
, respectively, for the two production processes.
Similarly, a three-dimensional fit was performed in the framework of simplified template cross sections.
Results are reported for the
VBF
cross section in an inclusive phase space and

\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{g}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\text{F}
cross sections in two exclusive regions of phase space
defined by particle-level requirements on the Higgs-boson
p_{\text{T}}
.
All measurements are consistent with SM predictions.

Appendix: Distributions of
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
in signal regions

Figures

12
and
13
show the
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distributions in all signal regions with background predictions adjusted by the likelihood fit.

Figure 12:
Observed and expected
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distributions as used in the fit in all signal regions (SRs) in the
VBF
category for the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
(left),
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
(middle) and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
(right) analysis channels.
The bottom panels show the ratio of observed data events to expected background
events (black points).
The observed Higgs-boson signal (
\mu=1.09
) is shown with the solid red line.
Entries with values that would exceed the
x
-axis range are shown in the last bin of each distribution.
The signal and background predictions are determined in the likelihood fit.
The size of the combined statistical, experimental and theoretical uncertainties in the background is indicated by the hatched bands.

Figure 13:
Observed and expected
m_{\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\text{MMC}}
distributions as used in the fit in all signal regions (SRs) in the boosted
category for the
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
(left),
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{lep}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
(middle) and
\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}{}\mathit{{\tau}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{\text{had}}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
(right) analysis channels.
The bottom panels show the ratio of observed data events to expected background
events (black points).
The observed Higgs-boson signal (
\mu=1.09
) is shown with the solid red line.
Entries with values that would exceed the
x
-axis range are shown in the last bin of each distribution.
The signal and background predictions are determined in the likelihood fit.
The size of the combined statistical, experimental and theoretical uncertainties in the background is indicated by the hatched bands.
